# LEDGER — the durable record under PROTOCOL v4

Coordinator-only. Append-only. ONE LINE per ruling, landing or stamp, oldest first, in the form
`YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM · KIND · SHA · one sentence` where KIND is RULING, LANDING or STAMP.
A lane's tick reads the tail since its last tick — a few lines — and nothing else of the channel;
`.claude/coord-scripts/fleet-read.sh <LANE> [SINCE]` prints exactly those lines after the inbox.
Never rewrite a line: a correction is a NEW line that names the line it corrects.

2026-09-23 03:37 · RULING · 718060141d · O4 RULED -- none of the six keeps alloc-count-semantics. Every one reads COUNT (TSV:98/99/102/113/120/126) or is COUNT by construction: each failing leg mints a counted golib object on every run, so testing.cs:750 takes the COUNT arm. strings TestBuilderGrowSizeclasses, slices TestGrow, io TestPipeAllocations, strings TestBuilderGrow and context TestAllocs become DEFERRED. testing TestAllocsPerRun becomes STRUCTURAL on a RE-GROUNDED proof, not the edwards/nistec analogy (which X(3) reverses). In Go too, `global = new(T)` escapes into `any` and costs one malloc (allocs_test.go:9-20). For an unmanaged T the managed pointer needs address-stable storage, because through the interface the pointer is reachable by reflect as an integer. Go's non-moving heap provides that for free; the CLR's compacting GC does not. The eager pinnable T[1] slot (ж.StandardBox.cs:61-67; AllocationCounter.cs:39-42) is the object Go never allocates. A pinned self-box roots itself and leaks, and a lazily created slot breaks heap()'s ref alias (DESIGN-zh-box-reduction.md:444-452). The proof must also answer the two nameable alternatives: a pointer that IS the pinned array, and whole-program X3 analysis selecting a slot-free kind (refused, because the pointer flows into `any`). No floor field, no signature re-sign (count-free convention, 04:57), stale lines dropped. slices TestConcat STAYS alloc-count-semantics (leg 1 reads BYTES 168 B/run, TSV:101), with the reason naming legs 2-4 as COUNT (Grow's make plus append) under REC-C §B. Plan corrections: io's 14 = 1 box + 3 channels x 4 (channel.cs:363-368) + 1 field-ref box, so its plan is REC-G (owner R), not zh-box. The strings.Builder box sits at the identity-keyed boundary (copyCheck stores and compares its own address, builder.cs:26-40; vetoes X1/X2 zh:162-165, XM-6 b-prime:583-585, three-capabilities §6), so zh-box is not its plan. Its floor claim stays in prose until MakeNoZero's buffer is counted: bytealg_impl.cs:24 is uncounted, and a floor must share the counter's unit (SKILL:850-858). The ElemRefBox from String() (builder.cs:44 -> unsafe.cs:985) goes to REC-D. strings TestBuilderAllocs and bufio TestReadStringAllocs, already deferred at 718060141d, are re-signed with the corrected attribution. context is written in the fmt partial form: B′ is named for the receiver field-ref boxes at context.cs:700/:709/:711, with the field-view cache possibly collapsing :709/:711 stated; the surplus is unattributed; G's B′-admission read is post-hop. 2026-09-23 22:36 · FINDING · 020365abcd · G's post-hop (1): claude/g-posthop-wsl-loopback 020365abcd on fa18863b94. The first SHA G sent, 8e1d0ea8f4, named no commit; G corrected it, and it was read back by ls-remote. crypto/tls's LINUX FOURTH STATE is ATTRIBUTED. WSL's generated /etc/hosts maps localhost to 127.0.0.1 ONLY, while BoringSSL's runner listens on [::1] (shim_dispatcher.go:37). Go's shim discards the runner's -ipv6 flag (bogo_shim_test.go:62) and dials "localhost" (:286), so the connection is refused. PROOF, a namespace-local A/B with NO system change: a hosts copy with "::1 localhost" lets Go's own TestBogoSuite PASS (1,022 pass / 2,396 skip / 0 fail at R's full fan-out); the identical copy without it fails 769. Re-reading the row needs the host's resolution changed, which is an OWNER HAND; G correctly did not take it. It measures candidate mechanism 1, Go's -gcflags=-m at go1.24.13, over six members plus a control, with five findings. (1) A frame-resident var is SILENT even at -m=2 (formatBits' a, md5's digest), so for vars the rule inverts: only 'moved to heap' signals an escape, while make/new/literals keep refusal-on-silence. (2) The inlined-call-site precondition holds, per caller site; utf16's member reads 'does not escape' only in the TEST binary's build. (3) sha256 Sum's @new<Digest> is itself an inlined-call-site allocation, so the value-carrier sizing needs a re-read. (4) The oracle keys on the operator position, not ast Pos(). (5) An emitted body uses only its non-inlined answer. CROSS-LANE: finding (1) bears directly on C2's string-literal §8.2 escape-oracle join, and is routed to C2 with its revision 4. 2026-09-24 06:47 · OWNER ORDER · 086853edd5 · UP NEXT, by the owner (2026-09-24): FIX THE LINUX PACKAGING PROBLEM. The multi-target go.* packages give the compiler the windows flavour in lib/ (go.syscall's lib/net10.0/syscall.dll is byte-identical to win-x64), while the linux flavour sits under runtimes/linux-x64 where the compiler never sees it. So a converted program that reaches platform-divergent syscall surface (the README walkthrough's x/sys/unix) cannot build on Linux from nuget.org. It is one of the FIRST post-release items, run alongside the post-release trains and not behind them. Seat owner: G, the measuring lane, which takes it right after the in-flight len/cap seat. SCOPE: a per-RID compile surface, i.e. reference assemblies per RID (ref/ plus runtimes/, or RID-specific packages) or a RID-selected compile asset; size the options against how NuGet resolves compile-versus-runtime assets for a RID-less and a RID-specific build, and against the push-nuget L3 merge (37 multi-target packages). GATE: G's README walkthrough steps 1-4 GREEN on the WSL linux arm against the fixed packages, from a local feed first and nuget.org after the next publish, with the output byte-equal to go run; the Windows walkthrough stays green; release-nuget -VerifyOnly still passes. It ships in the next release (1.24.13.2), and the README platforms note and the Roadmap Platforms row are rewritten from that evidence.

2026-09-26 04:10 · ACCEPT · 7bb1332090 · P1's ITEM 1, claude/p1-runtime-sema 7bb1332090 (with its red arm 8a7f663a4, landed as a unit), is ACCEPTED for TRAIN C on R's review as park-seam owner: AGREES, read-only. (1) RuntimeSemaphore.Waiters takes ONLY the bucket monitor that Acquire (Monitor.Enter(b)) and Release (lock(b)) use, so no lock-order cycle is possible. The count runs from Enqueue to Dequeue under b, exactly Go's nwait window; a re-queued loser is counted again, as in Go; the TryGetValue-miss race reads 0 transiently, as Go's does, and TestSemaHandoff re-reads. Per word vs Go's per root is correct for the only reader. (2) Dropping lifo and the profile flags costs NO live caller: the only passers are the linkname push functions in runtime/sema.cs:97-124, dead in the corpus because sync, internal/sync and internal/poll hand-own their pulls straight onto RuntimeSemaphore; runtime's own callers (worldsema, gcsema, metricsSema, traceAdvanceSema, handoff=true at proc.cs:1467) all pass lifo=false. (3) TestSemaHandoff will FAIL its direct-handoff ratio, probably near 0 of 10,000: the releaser's CAS runs right after Signal.Set, before the waiter's OS wake, and GOMAXPROCS does not bound threads here. The FIX is R's FOLLOW-UP, accepted: after a handoff Release, a bounded wait (spin, then Thread.Yield) until the handed-off waiter reports RUNNING, the managed form of goyield. It touches handoff=true releases only (Mutex starvation, worldsema, traceAdvanceSema), and its gate measures the releaser's added latency and the ratio before and after. CORRECTION ROUTED TO P2: sync.Mutex contention never reaches semrelease1, so I4 (mutexevent) must hook RuntimeSemaphore or the pull companions, which is the park seam, reviewed by R.

The build happens on the metrics side because runtime cannot name runtime/metrics' type (the reverse would be a cycle). The stated divergence is unchanged: Go reuses a Value's histogram storage "when possible", and the crossing already allocates fresh per sample. RED FIRST: a GolibTests arm reading /gc/heap/allocs-by-size:bytes through metrics.Read and summing Counts. 2026-09-26 14:54 · RULING · 7433c7ac38 · The i9 SIZED the WINDOWS runtime.Gosched slowness (a scratch probe driving the REAL GoschedBackoff.Yield, plus shapes differing only in the escalation step). ROOTED: every Gosched is a timed Thread.Yield, a yield is INERT if it did not switch or took under 2 µs, and the 64th consecutive inert yield escalates to Thread.Sleep(1). A lone yielder is inert every time, so it sleeps every 64 calls, and on Windows 11 that "1 ms" sleep is 15.7 ms (per-process timer resolution; the machine-wide 1 ms reading is misleading). 1M lone yields take 237 s on windows (p50 0.10 µs, p99 15.5 ms); TestGoroutineSwitch's 10 x 1M is about 2,376 s, past the 1,800 s package deadline: the 32-absence stall. Linux's Sleep(1) is about 1 ms, which is why linux passes. The escalation's reason (the BOARD's linux CFS starvation finding) does not bite on windows: a strict token ring finishes under every shape, bare Thread.Yield included. SHAPES (1M lone yields): S0 today 237.2 s; S1 bare Thread.Yield 0.1 s; S2 Sleep(0) 0.1 s (not a linux fix); S3 timeBeginPeriod(1) 30.4 s (2 ms, process-wide; rejected); S4a a HIGH-RESOLUTION waitable timer at 1 ms (CREATE_WAITABLE_TIMER_HIGH_RESOLUTION, as time_impl already uses) 24.0 s, p99 1.5 ms; S4b a high-res 100 µs 8.5 s; S5 threshold 1024 13.6 s (still 15.7 ms). RULED: S4a, on WINDOWS, as the FAITHFUL realization of the ratified design ("escalate to a 1 ms sleep"): the same threshold and the same sleep length, on the timer golib/time already trusts. Linux is unchanged (its Sleep(1) already delivers about 1 ms). S1-on-windows is NOT taken. It would match Go's per-call cost, but thread-per-goroutine means many spinning goroutines burn many cores where Go shares GOMAXPROCS threads, and nothing has measured that case; revisit only with that measurement. The i9 CUTS S4a on master 7433c7ac38, red first with a bounded arm that the 15.7 ms sleep fails and the high-res timer passes, with its timer lifetime per thread classified in R's thread-state census. GATES: GolibTests, and a windows runtime/pprof reading where TestGoroutineSwitch completes inside the deadline and the 32 absent leaves report verdicts; sync, context and time sweeps unchanged.

2026-09-26 15:47 · RULING · 7433c7ac38 · P2 SIZED the windows-only Mapping:null item (read-only). WHY: the TEST picks its addresses per OS (proto_test.go testPCs). On linux they are the Starts of the first two /proc/self/maps entries, so synthetic PCs are never involved; on windows, darwin and ios they are abi.FuncPCABIInternal(f1/f2), which here are SYNTHETIC PCs (the band from 0xFFFF_8000_0000_0000), against readMainModuleMapping()'s real exe range, so no mapping contains them. The same holds for every REAL CPU or heap profile on all three OSes: a synthetic PC gets no mapping (only the windows and darwin test arms check it). Darwin is PREDICTED to fail the same four leaves (not measured). RULED: option (a), a SYNTHETIC TEXT MAPPING, and it goes FIRST in P2's queue, ahead of magnitude, because windows is the banking host and (a) moves 4 of runtime/pprof's windows leaves where magnitude moves 1. On windows and darwin, readMainModuleMapping returns the synthetic text range (from the caller-span base 0x8000_8000_0000_0000 through the synthetic-PC band) with the real exe path and build ID, and readMapping lists that entry FIRST (ID 1), followed by the real modules unchanged. On linux the same entry is APPENDED after /proc/self/maps, so the linux test's IDs 1 and 2 are unchanged. The mapping STATES A FACT: the tokens are where this runtime's Go text lives, the range sits in the non-canonical and kernel half so no real module overlaps it, and real profiles' Go frames gain a mapping on every OS. Cost: hand-owning readMapping and readMainModuleMapping in proto_windows.cs, proto_darwin.cs and proto_other.cs (host facts, the right place for a hand-own), plus ONE golib or runtime accessor naming the band's bounds (the two bases live in two assemblies today), about 60 lines, red first. CHECKS OWED in the seat: the range must not claim the TAGGED-TOKEN space as text in a way any consumer reads back (profiles carry stack PCs, not data tokens; state it); a profile written with the new mapping must still round-trip through the converted profile parser (TestConvertCPUProfile and TestConvertMemProfile themselves); and on linux, TestProcSelfMaps and the linux arms must be unchanged.
2026-09-26 15:48 · RULING · 7433c7ac38 · P2 SIZED magnitude (TestCPUProfileMultithreadMagnitude/parallel; read-only, linux, 4 cores). On linux Go's own test turns the failure into a skip (golang.org/issue/13841). MECHANISM, inferred and not yet instrumented: EventPipe's SampleProfiler records every thread in managed code about every 1 ms without asking whether it was ON a CPU, so a runnable-but-not-running hog thread is still sampled; per-thread thinning then keeps one sample per 10 ms of WALL time (4 x ~5 s = ~20 s) while the threads got ~16.4 s of CPU. /serial passes because one hog has a core to itself. RULED: the fix shape is APPROVED, SECOND in P2's queue after the synthetic text mapping. Weight each thread by its REAL CPU time: the sampler polls per-thread CPU time for the session's threads (linux /proc/self/task/<tid>/stat utime+stime; windows GetThreadTimes; darwin thread_info THREAD_BASIC_INFO), and at Stop keeps round(cpuTime x hz) of each thread's samples, evenly chosen, in place of one per wall slot. EventPipe's ThreadID is the OS thread id, so the join is direct. A thread exiting between polls loses at most one interval (100 ms; stated). The companion only (about 120 lines); the default build is untouched, keeping the owner's opt-in rule. Red: a GolibTests arm (N hogs on N+1 threads, samples x period within 10% of their measured CPU time) plus /parallel's logged diff. INSTRUMENT the mechanism in the red arm (record on-CPU against runnable per sample if EventPipe exposes it, else the per-thread CPU against sample counts), so it is measured before the fix is claimed.

The assert needs the woken waiter's CAS to win at least 2/3 of 10,000 trials (Go is over 90%): Go's semrelease1 hands the waiter the ticket, readyWithTime puts it in runnext, and goyield() queues the releaser behind it on the same P. On P1's branch (semacquire1/semrelease1 over RuntimeSemaphore), Release(s, handoff) sets HandedOff, readies and signals the waiter, then RETURNS, so the releaser CASes in nanoseconds while the waiter is still waking (microseconds): the 1-8 per 10,000. SMALLEST FIX, APPROVED (golib RuntimeSemaphore.cs only): on a handoff that actually handed a permit (Go's s.ticket == 1), the releaser WAITS, BOUNDED, for the waiter to resume. The waiter sets a per-waiter Resumed event (spinCount 0) as the last act of Acquire, after its ParkScope dispose, and the bound is 10 ms, Go's forcePreemptNS, so a waiter that never resumes costs the bound and never deadlocks. R read the resume path: it takes no lock the releaser could hold. REACH: handoff=true comes only from internal/sync's Mutex unlockSlow in starvation mode and runtime's semrelease1(handoff) callers, so sync.Mutex starvation handoff becomes Go-faithful too (a rare path). CUT (a), STACKED on P1's claude/p1-runtime-sema TIP 959a88df38 (TRAIN E's first seat; the file already carries P1's Waiters() hunk, and the gate lives there). P1's proposal feeds Go's own state: GOMAXPROCS does procresize's bookkeeping verbatim (sched.totaltime, procresizetime, and the gomaxprocs global, which today stays at NumCPU); the pause delta from GC.GetTotalPauseDuration goes through accumulateGCPauseTime; idle is the remainder of the cycle's total less pause CPU and process CPU, floored at 0; accumulate, accumulateGCPauseTime and compute stay auto-converted. THE UPDATE POINT, RULED: neither (a) runtime.GC() only nor (b) a callback on every observed gen2. Option (a) is REJECTED as the final shape: a program that never calls runtime.GC() would read every /cpu/classes metric as 0 for ever, and ordinary monitoring (a metrics exporter reading runtime/metrics) does exactly that, so it does not run like Go. Option (b) adds a golib-to-runtime callback on the finalizer thread and a new lock against metrics.Read. RULED (c): a LAZY CATCH-UP at the read point. Before cpuStats is copied for a reader, if the CLR's gen2 collection count has advanced since the last accounting, account the interval since then (total, pause, process CPU) in one step. This keeps Go's semantics (the values change only when a cycle has completed, and every completed cycle counts), runs on the reader's thread under the lock the read path already holds, and needs no callback. runtime.GC() ends with the same catch-up. The hook point is P1's choice: the smallest faithful one, which may be a one-row hand-own of cpuStatsAggregate.compute (then the two-seeded emission footprint and CNR are owed); if no read-path hook is faithful, P1 reports why and (b) is the fallback. COST: measure the catch-up on the metrics.Read path (no gen2 since the last read is one counter read).

The SIGCHLD=SIG_IGN / SA_NOCLDWAIT auto-reap caveat is now stated at that site. pidfd_spawn is not used because glibc 2.35, the only linux C library in the fleet, does not export it, so that branch could never execute. (ii) checkClonePidfd does NOT spawn a probe child. The ordered probe was cut first and MEASURED WRONG: os/exec's TestSIGCHLD went red ("too many SIGCHLD signals"), because Go's probe clones with exit signal 0 (CLONE_VFORK|CLONE_VM|CLONE_PIDFD, reaped with WCLONE) while a posix_spawn child always raises SIGCHLD, at the first StartProcess of a program that is watching SIGCHLD. The body answers nil, with the argument at the site: checkPidfd's own first three steps prove pidfd_open, waitid(P_PIDFD) and pidfd_send_signal. Go needs a fourth step only because it mints a child's pidfd through a different door (clone(CLONE_PIDFD)) that a filter can refuse separately; our spawn mints it through the pidfd_open that step 1 proved. This is not a vacuous pass: the two os tests then run the real pidfd paths and pass. ARMS: LinuxPidfdTests (a spawned child's pidfd signals it, and waitid(P_PIDFD) reaps it as CLD_KILLED). RED FIRST: the two os pidfd tests SKIP at the union without the commit and PASS with it. 2026-09-27 01:48 · RULING · 448efc4f0b · G SIZED the darwin libc-funnel marshal (census at 448efc4f0b, the load-bearing lines verified by G). Every darwin syscall reaches ONE funnel, syscall/darwin's `call` into golib GoLibcCall.Call (a delegate* unmanaged over the libc address), and it does no token check. So a pointer to a reference-bearing Go struct reaches libc as an order token and fails with EFAULT: a direct syscall.Stat(path, &local) does so today, and after G's (b) os.Stat's &fs.sys does too, where before (b) it was a raw unpinned address and silently corrupted. POPULATION: 17 marshallable typed wrappers (Stat_t, Statfs_t, Timeval, FdSet, Rusage, RawSockaddrAny), about 9 unsafe.Pointer sockopt/bpf sites, and internal/syscall/unix's ResState. Pointer-bearing: Msghdr, Kevent_t (Udata), BpfProgram, Iovec. Left alone: non-struct pointees and the 9-argument Getnameinfo. DESIGN APPROVED, ONE SEAT, CUT NOW: (1) golib generalizes NativeStructMarshal's core from the fixed six-argument invoker to a span of arguments (scratch widened to 9), with the linux keystone's entry kept as a thin adapter; (2) GoLibcCall gains a marshalled call (tokens to Go-layout native copies, invoke, decode back on success AND failure, one buffer per distinct token); (3) darwin's `call` routes through it, darwin-only. Layout comes from GoFieldOffsets of the darwin-emitted types, as on linux. POINTER-BEARING STRUCTS, RULED (b): a NIL pointer field encodes as 0, which is exactly what Go passes; a non-nil pointer field leaves that argument's token in place, so libc answers EFAULT, as (a) does. The linux-style panic (c) is REJECTED for darwin, because it would turn a kqueue or sendmsg errno into a crash. Named follow-ups, each its own sizing: Msghdr and Iovec as hand-own mirrors; and Kevent_t.Udata, which the kernel never dereferences and hands back verbatim, so a token there may round-trip exactly as Go's opaque pointer does. MEASUREMENT LIMIT, accepted: no fleet host runs darwin, so the proof is host-neutral golib arms (an [UnmanagedCallersOnly] fake libc function handed to GoLibcCall writes darwin Stat_t bytes at darwin offsets and is decoded through the real funnel code; red first, the fake receives the token number today), plus H7 darwin compiling the wiring. GATES: GolibTests both flavours; the full behavioral suite (a golib API change); H7 x3; the linux keystone's regression through os, syscall and net on linux at the roster (its entry becomes an adapter); CNR if the converter moves. It joins TRAIN F if it is gated before TRAIN F assembles, otherwise the train after.

2026-09-27 02:37 · RULING · 448efc4f0b · R SIZED its seven runtime claims (worktree branch claude/r-runtime-claims on 448efc4f0b; nothing pushed). RULINGS. (1) minmax (TestMaxFloat, TestMinFloat): cut locally as ce81348728; golib's floating min/max breaks an EQUAL compare by the sign bit (Go: max(-0, 0) = 0, min(-0, 0) = -0); MinMaxSignedZeroTests red first (3 of 5 at base; float32 passed by argument-order luck, stated). APPROVED as is. (2) TestFloat64 is a CONVERTER DEFECT, not softfloat's: a COMPOUND shift-assign with a variable count emits a native `x >>= (int)(n)`, and C# masks the count (664 reads as 24), while Go gives 0, or sign fill for a signed right shift, once the count reaches the width. The binary form is already guarded (`.Lsh`/`.Rsh` through the shift-guard predicates); the two compound arms in visitAssignStmt never consult them. DESIGN APPROVED: route SHL_ASSIGN/SHR_ASSIGN through the same guard predicates; when guarded, emit a golib `ref this` extension (`lhs.RshAssign(count)` / `LshAssign`), so the left side is evaluated ONCE, as Go does (fields, locals and ref-returning element indexers all bind); a provably bounded count stays native. FOOTPRINT: about 62 compound-shift lines in the committed corpus carry a non-literal count (compress/flate's and compress/lzw's bit readers among them); constant counts stay native. OWED: the two-seeded three-target emission footprint predicted in writing first, CNR, the new symbols in symbols.json (then gensymbols), the unfiltered `go test ./...`, and a COST reading on a flate- or lzw-heavy program before and after (Go's own compiler emits the same bounds check unless it proves the count small, so parity is expected; measure it). (3) TestBigItems: an array KEY aliases. `m[key] = key` clones the stored VALUE but not the KEY, so every stored array key shares the one buffer the loop mutates. RULED GOLIB-SIDE, as R leans: golib map<K,V> clones a value-clone key (an IArray, or a struct that carries one through its clone) on INSERT of a NEW key. That is one place, it covers reflect's SetMapIndex and composite literals too, and it costs one allocation per new array-keyed insert, which is Go's own key copy; the converter stays unchanged. ALSO READ the key READ-OUT paths (range over the map, reflect MapKeys and MapIter.Key): in Go those hand out copies, so mutating a range key must not mutate the stored key. If they alias, the same seat covers them, sized first. (4) TestPanicNil: runtime GODEBUG plumbing (the PanicNilError substitution and the panicnil non-default metric, gated on debug.panicnil), fixable; proceed with the focused debug run. (5) The defer pair (TestRecoverMatching, TestIssue43920): golib's recover reads one thread-local captured panic slot with no frame matching, while Go lets recover succeed only in a deferred call made by the panicking frame and nests replacing panics. It is the GoFrame recover model, the deepest of the seven: SIZE with a written design before any cut, after 2-4. SEAT SHAPE: TWO seats, not one stack, so a converter footprint question cannot hold the rest: (i) the converter compound-shift seat; (ii) the golib/runtime seat (minmax, the map-key clone, panicnil). Each is gated at its own tip: runtime -tests filtered to its claimed names on windows and linux, GolibTests both flavours, and the full behavioral suite for any golib API change. ORDER as R proposed: minmax gates now, then 2 and 3, then 4, then 5.

THE JUDGMENT, RULED as P1 proposes: heapObjectsCanMove answers false. The CLR compacts, but every address a converted program can hold (an unsafe.Pointer or a uintptr taken from a heap object) is a pinned address or a stable token, never a movable CLR address, so no Go-visible address moves, which is exactly the property the function reports. The row's comment states the reason. CNR is owed at the union, as before.

2026-09-27 12:03 · RULING · a7dc4570d3 · THE PANIC-PATH FRAME DESIGN (R's DESIGN-panic-stack-frames.md, claude/r-panic-frames-design a7dc4570d3; P2 reviewed the frame half, inbox e3a5725cff) is APPROVED as option B with P2's FOUR CHANGES, to be cut AFTER M3 (it lands on M3's reshaped walk). THE DESIGN: splice Go's LOGICAL panicking stack into captureCallers, with no emission change: a per-thread stack of deferred sequences, a per-panic own-site trace, and a per-panic kind (a fault gives panicmem and sigpanic, a divide gives panicdivide); GoFrame.Run is NoInlining. Option A (running defers inside the exception filter) is rejected on R's evidence: a panic thrown inside a filter is swallowed by the CLR, cleanup order inverts against non-Go finally blocks, and it needs an emission change in every deferring function. Disclosure is kept only for deferreturn (the WithLoop form). GO'S OWN ANSWER, measured by P2 (linux amd64, go1.24.13, Callers from the deferred call after recover): plain panic gives func1, runtime.gopanic panic.go:792, the test, tRunner, goexit; a nil dereference adds runtime.panicmem panic.go:262 and runtime.sigpanic signal_unix.go:925 above the test at its faulting line; a divide adds runtime.panicdivide panic.go:241; a nil deferred func in a loop adds runtime.deferreturn panic.go:610. THE CHANGES, all required in the cut: (1) PAIRING: push one entry for EVERY GoFrame.Run activation that runs deferred calls (its m_count > 0 branch), holding that frame's OWN panic or null, because a Run frame is on the stack for a normal return too. Positional pairing mis-splices one sequence too high when a panic's deferred call reaches a helper that defers and calls Callers. When a deferred call's panic REPLACES the handled one inside one Run (the aborted-panic shape), that entry is updated. The record's "nothing on the no-panic path" becomes "one push and pop per deferring return, beside the existing save and restore". (2) OWNER FRAME: SiteTrace is the raised exception's OWN trace, as caught by the owning frame, never the inherited PanicTrace. Its last Go frame is the owner and is dropped, with an ASSERTION that it equals the next live Go frame of the walk; on a mismatch nothing is spliced and today's stack is answered, because a missing splice is a known divergence and a wrong one is a silent lie. (3) Spliced frames go through M3's SAME skip and capacity path and are interned with internRootFrame (one PC per Go call site, GOROOT-relative file names); R's red arms are read on the M3 base. (4) Run frames are matched by MethodBase identity, never by name; a live walk of the converted runtime.gopanic interning a second record is named at the site. COVERAGE: TestCallersPanic, DoublePanic, NilPointerPanic, DivZeroPanic and DeferNilFuncPanic; the WithLoop residual stays a disclosure, and if the converter's hint is ever cut it states Go's open-coding rule in full. Not covered, stated: CPU-profile stacks sampled inside a deferred call (the sampler walks through EventPipe, not captureCallers). ORDER: R's seat (iii), then P2's M3, then B (R), with P2 reviewing the cut.
2026-09-27 12:07 · RULING · f8dfffc99a · R SIZED the DOTTED PACKAGE PATH (read-only, at f8dfffc99a): a '.' inside an import-path SEGMENT is lost on readback. The converter flattens an import path into a C# namespace by joining segments with '.', keeping the in-segment dots, and [GoPackage] carries only the package NAME, so no lossless path exists at run time. Three independent decoders replace '.' with '/' (reflect's GoPackagePath in GoReflect.TypeNaming.cs, GoSyntheticPC's GoNameOf, and runtime's goFrameName), and GoStructSynthesis.containerFor is the mirror encoder. Go says vendor/golang.org/x/crypto/chacha20; we say vendor/golang/org/x/crypto/chacha20. CONSUMERS that see the lossy value: reflect's PkgPath on types, methods and fields; reflectlite; generic type-argument names; runtime Frame.Function, Callers and FuncForPC names; traceback and debug.Stack text including "created by"; gob Register names. Not affected: %T and Type.String() (package name only), and equality-only uses. POPULATION: in the standard library, 19 vendor packages, and R's estimate is 0 banked verdicts today (no in-scope test asserts a dotted path; a vendor frame in a compared traceback was looked for and not found, UNVERIFIED). The REAL population is user modules: every github.com/..., golang.org/x/... or similar path has a dot in its first segment, so an estimated 100% of third-party conversions get wrong PkgPath, gob names and stack names. RULED: OPTION A, cut by R after its parsedebugvars sizing and before the panic-frames cut (which waits for M3). [GoPackage] gains the VERBATIM import path, and the three decoders plus containerFor read it FIRST, all in ONE seat, so reflect and runtime never disagree. It is emitted conditionally, only where the path is not recoverable from the namespace (an in-segment dot, a /vN suffix, a package name that differs from its directory), which also cures the recorded /vN and name-versus-directory losses. CONDITIONS: (1) the convergence rule for packageInfoWriter, which copies an existing package_info.cs outside its marker sections: the attribute is REGENERATED on every reconvert (inside a writer-owned section or its own rewrite rule), with an arm proving a stale path is replaced; (2) RED FIRST with a NEW behavioral test whose module path is dotted (for example example.com/dotted/v2): reflect PkgPath, runtime.Caller's function name, FuncForPC and gob's registered name all byte-equal to Go's output; (3) the two-seeded three-target corpus footprint predicted first (about 20 package_info.cs files plus any test bridges); CNR; GolibTests both flavours; the reflect row and the importer canaries recomputed at gate time; runtime's Callers-filtered read unchanged. Options B (escape the dot in namespaces: over 1,400 files and user-visible C# names, and it does not cure /vN) and C (golib-only, vendor-only) are REJECTED.

The test fails with "want function runtime_test.I.M, got" and an empty name, for two reasons. (1) reflect's func Value.Pointer() answers a delegate-method token; FuncForPC resolves it, but Frames.Next does not, so the two disagree about one pc. (2) That pc names the converter's lambda for a method expression, spelled TestCallersFromWrapper.func1, where Go names its autogenerated WRAPPER, runtime_test.I.M. RULED: CUT as sized. (a) runtime's callerFrameRecord gains the delegate method behind a reflect func token as a third source, cached per method, so FuncForPC and CallersFrames give one answer; reflect is unchanged. (b) golib gains GoWrapperAttribute(goName), stamped by the converter on the two lambda forms convSelectorExpr emits for method expressions: "I.M" for the interface form and "(*T).M" for the pointer-of-value form. The method-group form is the method itself, as in Go, and stays unmarked. (c) A marked method is named pkg.<goName> at File "<autogenerated>", Line 1, Go's answer for a wrapper pc. (d) It is ELIDED from Callers, as Go's tracebackPCs elides a wrapper whose callee is an ordinary function. RULE ADDED TO B (R's panic-frames cut): Go KEEPS a wrapper frame whose callee is gopanic, sigpanic or panicwrap (elideWrapperCalling), so B's splice keeps a GoWrapper frame that is the panic site. TestStackWrapperStackPanic's subtests move with B, not F. PREDICTION: TestCallersFromWrapper fail to pass and nothing else moves; TestStackWrapperStackInlinePanic stays a fail (inlining). The review used four finder lenses, each building the branch and base converters and converting probes, with two skeptics per finding. D1 was INTRODUCED by the cut: the paren elision leaves emitGuardedShift's receiver unparenthesized, so the variable-count `(a+b) >> n` emits `(int8)((int8)(a + b).Rsh(n))`. The cast binds to the int32 Rsh result, silently wrong: 100 against Go's -28. D2: unary + and signed unary ^ are classified invariant and type-only, so overflowing operands leak into value consumers uncast. D3: `<<` is unnarrowed on the guarded variable-count path; the "emission already narrows" premise holds for constant counts only. D4: switch case values under an interface tag need a typed consumer. D5: a map key through a type parameter with a map core is a compile error. D6: byte/uint8 spelling through named func types. D8: unneeded casts on a guarded >> whose receiver is already narrow. D9: coverage; the "case value" arm is tagless, and there are no arms for a map-literal key, make len/cap, a 3-index max, a divisor, or variable-count shifts. RE-CUT red first, each arm red at ff71ab6a9e, then the footprint and CNR re-scored against a restated prediction. D7 is PRE-EXISTING and becomes its own seat: a unary minus or plus over a unary operand emits `--a` or `++a`, a C# pre-decrement or pre-increment that MUTATES the variable. C2's MinInt/-1 continues stacked; it merges the re-cut tip.
